## Exodus 29:1–37 (id: 11249)

Moses had to consecrate (or sanctify) Aaron and his sons to serve the Lord. The focus on making the priests holy appears throughout the ceremonies ([29:6](https://ref.ly/Exod29:6), [21](https://ref.ly/Exod29:21), [28](https://ref.ly/Exod29:28), [29](https://ref.ly/Exod29:29), [34](https://ref.ly/Exod29:34), [36](https://ref.ly/Exod29:36), [37](https://ref.ly/Exod29:37)). God set them apart not only for service. But he set them apart to serve a God whose nature is very different from that of sinful humans. [Leviticus 8](https://ref.ly/Lev8:1-Lev8:36) gives the account of how they followed these instructions.

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 29:1-37

## Exodus 29:4 (id: 11266)

*wash them with water:* This action showed an important truth. No one can live in God’s presence while carrying the filth of sin in their lives ([29:21](https://ref.ly/Exod29:21)).

The washing did not remove sin by itself. It was a sign. It showed that people must be made clean before coming near to God.

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 29:4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Aaron (ID: `person:Aaron`); Tent of Meeting (ID: `realia:TentOfMeeting`)

## Exodus 29:9 (id: 11268)

* *permanent:* The fulfillment of this promise, like the one to King David, clearly extends beyond human limits. Aaron behaved badly (for example, chapter [32](https://ref.ly/Exod32:1-Exod32:35); [Numbers 11](https://ref.ly/Num11:1-Num11:35)). His sons and descendants were no better ([1 Samuel 2:12–17](https://ref.ly/1Sam2:12-1Sam2:17); [Isaiah 28:7](https://ref.ly/Isa28:7); [Ezekiel 8:15–16](https://ref.ly/Ezek8:15-Ezek8:16); [Malachi 2:1–9](https://ref.ly/Mal2:1-Mal2:9)). The promise could only find its ultimate fulfillment in Jesus Christ (see [Hebrews 7:26](https://ref.ly/Heb7:26); [9:11](https://ref.ly/Heb9:11)).
* *to ordain* (literally *you will fill the hands of*): This means to give a person a task to perform.

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 29:9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Sash (ID: `realia:Sash`); Aaron (ID: `person:Aaron`); Priesthood (ID: `keyterm:Priesthood`); Turban (ID: `realia:Turban`)

## Exodus 29:10–34 (id: 11250)

The consecration of Aaron's priesthood involved:

* a sin offering ([29:10–14](https://ref.ly/Exod29:10-Exod29:14)),
* a burnt offering ([29:15–18](https://ref.ly/Exod29:15-Exod29:18)), and
* an ordination offering ([29:19–28](https://ref.ly/Exod29:19-Exod29:28)).

These patterns were later used for the people's regular offerings (see [29:38–46](https://ref.ly/Exod29:38-Exod29:46); [Leviticus 1–5](https://ref.ly/Lev1:1-Lev5:19)). In each case, Aaron and his sons placed their hands on the heads of the sacrificial animals ([Exodus 29:10](https://ref.ly/Exod29:10), [15](https://ref.ly/Exod29:15), [19](https://ref.ly/Exod29:19)). This act showed that sin is a matter of life and death. God will only remove sin by a death. 

Because it represents life, blood was important in these ceremonies ([29:12](https://ref.ly/Exod29:12), [16](https://ref.ly/Exod29:16), [20](https://ref.ly/Exod29:20), [21](https://ref.ly/Exod29:21)).

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 29:10-34

## Exodus 29:18 (id: 11254)

*pleasing aroma:* Such language is *anthropomorphism,* describing God with human terms and experiences. God’s delight in an appropriately offered sacrifice is like a human’s enjoyment of a pleasing smell.

## Exodus 29:19–28 (id: 11255)

This ordination offering is very similar to the peace offering, which is also called the fellowship offering ([Leviticus 3](https://ref.ly/Lev3:1-Lev3:17)). 

In this offering, the fat was burned as a gift to the Lord. The breast and the thigh were first lifted up before the Lord as a sign that they belonged to him ([29:24–26](https://ref.ly/Exod29:24-Exod29:26)). After that, they became the portion for the priests to eat ([29:26–28](https://ref.ly/Exod29:26-Exod29:28)). 

In a regular peace offering, the person who brought the sacrifice also ate part of the meat. This meal was a fellowship meal shared in God’s presence ([Leviticus 7:11–18](https://ref.ly/Lev7:11-Lev7:18)). In this case, the priests ate the meal as part of their ordination ([Exodus 29:31–34](https://ref.ly/Exod29:31-Exod29:34)).

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 29:19-28

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Aaron (ID: `person:Aaron`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Offering (ID: `keyterm:Offering`); Sheep (ID: `fauna:Lamb`); Priesthood (ID: `keyterm:Priesthood`); Altar (ID: `realia:Altar`); Temple Altar (ID: `realia:TempleAltar`); Stone Altar (ID: `realia:StoneAltar`); Tabernacle Altar (ID: `realia:TabernacleAltar`); Holy (ID: `keyterm:Holy`); Olive Oil (ID: `realia:OliveOil`); Anointing Oil (ID: `realia:AnointingOil`); Decree (ID: `keyterm:Decree`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Slaughtering (ID: `keyterm:Slaughtering`); Pleasing Odor (ID: `keyterm:PleasingOdor`); Basket (ID: `realia:Basket`); Unleavened Bread (ID: `realia:UnleavenedBread`); Cake (ID: `realia:Cake`)

## Exodus 29:20–21 (id: 11257)

The placing of the blood on the earlobes*,* thumbs*,* and big toes represented dedication of the entire person to God. Sprinkling it on their clothes indicated that the clothing, too, could be used only for holy purposes.

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 29:20-21

## Exodus 29:33 (id: 11261)

*atonement:* Traditionally, *atonement* has often been explained as a “covering over.” This means that sin is covered so it no longer stands between God and people.

Some modern scholars connect the word to another Semitic root (a related word family from ancient Middle Eastern languages). That root does not appear elsewhere in the Hebrew Bible. Based on that idea, they understand the word to mean “blot out,” “erase,” or “cleanse.”

In both views, the result is the same. The effects of sin are removed. Because of this, people and even sacred objects can remain in God’s presence without being destroyed.

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 29:33

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Appease (ID: `keyterm:Appease`); Holy (ID: `keyterm:Holy`)

## Exodus 30:12 (id: 11290)

*no plague:* Compare [2 Samuel 24](https://ref.ly/2Sam24:1-2Sam24:25), where a plague followed King David’s unauthorized census. A king would often take a census for reasons of pride or control. Here the people are giving God a ransom for themselves. This standard payment would go into the tabernacle treasury and initially be used in the construction of the tabernacle (see [Exodus 38:25–26](https://ref.ly/Exod38:25-Exod38:26)).

## Exodus 31:3 (id: 11300)

*I have filled him with the Spirit of God:* This is one of the earliest references to being filled with the Spirit as an expression of divine empowerment for activities that are clearly beyond normal human abilities (see also [Genesis 41:38](https://ref.ly/Gen41:38); [Numbers 11:17](https://ref.ly/Num11:17); [Judges 6:34](https://ref.ly/Judg6:34); [14:19](https://ref.ly/Judg14:19); [1 Samuel 10:6](https://ref.ly/1Sam10:6); [16:13](https://ref.ly/1Sam16:13); [Joel 2:28–29](https://ref.ly/Joel2:28-Joel2:29); [Micah 3:8](https://ref.ly/Mic3:8)).

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 31:3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Understanding (ID: `keyterm:Understanding`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Intention (ID: `keyterm:Intention`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Exodus 31:6 (id: 11301)

God gave detailed and remarkable instructions. He also provided skilled craftsmen who could complete the work exactly as he commanded.

In this way, God supplied what his people needed to obey him. This idea is similar to a prayer by Augustine in his book *Confessions*: “Give what you command, and command what you will” (10\.29\). Augustine meant that God must give people the ability to do what he requires.

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 31:6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Oholiab (ID: `person:Oholiab`); Ahisamach (ID: `person:Ahisamach`); Dan (ID: `place:Dan`); Tribe of Dan (ID: `group:Dan`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`)

## Exodus 31:11 (id: 11296)

The main idea of this whole section ([25:1–40:38](https://ref.ly/Exod25:1-Exod40:38)) is clear. God wanted to meet his people’s need for a visible sign of his presence. They needed something they could see that reminded them that he was with them.

However, this had to be done exactly as God commanded. God set the way his people were to approach him. He determines the terms by which people come to him, not human beings.

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 31:11

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Holy Place (ID: `place:HolyPlace`); Holy (ID: `keyterm:Holy`); Olive Oil (ID: `realia:OliveOil`); Anointing Oil (ID: `realia:AnointingOil`)

## Exodus 31:12–18 (id: 11297)

The Sabbath was understood to be the unique sign of the covenant ([31:13](https://ref.ly/Exod31:13), [16](https://ref.ly/Exod31:16), [17](https://ref.ly/Exod31:17); see also [20:8–11](https://ref.ly/Exod20:8-Exod20:11); [Isaiah 58:13–14](https://ref.ly/Isa58:13-Isa58:14); [Jeremiah 17:21–27](https://ref.ly/Jer17:21-Jer17:27); [Nehemiah 13:15–22](https://ref.ly/Neh13:15-Neh13:22)). The Sabbath also played an important role in God’s plan to make his people holy. *Holy* refers to being set apart for God’s special use and sharing his character ([Exodus 31:13](https://ref.ly/Exod31:13), [14](https://ref.ly/Exod31:14), [15](https://ref.ly/Exod31:15)). 

By resting on the Sabbath, the people showed that they belonged to him. They stopped their own work on that day ([31:15](https://ref.ly/Exod31:15), [17](https://ref.ly/Exod31:17)). 

This rest was also a practical way to show trust in God. It showed that they depended on him to provide what they needed (see [20:8–11](https://ref.ly/Exod20:8-Exod20:11) and study notes).

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 31:12-18
* **Associated Articles:** Exodus 20:8-11 (ID: `11157@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Sabbath (ID: `keyterm:Sabbath`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Holy (ID: `keyterm:Holy`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Mount Sinai (ID: `place:SinaiMount`); Testimony (ID: `keyterm:Witness.2`); To Dishonor (ID: `keyterm:Dishonor`); Covenant (ID: `keyterm:Covenant`); Tablet (ID: `realia:Tablet`)

## Exodus 31:14–15 (id: 11298)

It is very important to learn that we find life in God only by coming to him in his way.

This truth is made clear by the death penalty for disobedience (see also [Numbers 15:32–36](https://ref.ly/Num15:32-Num15:36)). This does not mean that God’s people must punish these sins in the same way. But at the beginning, God needed to make this truth clear (see [Acts 5:1–11](https://ref.ly/Acts5:1-Acts5:11)). 

If people do not understand this spiritual lesson, ritual obedience will not help them. Ritual obedience means simply following religious rules. It has no value without true faith and trust in God (see [Colossians 2:16–19](https://ref.ly/Col2:16-Col2:19)).

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 31:14-15

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Sabbath (ID: `keyterm:Sabbath`); Holy (ID: `keyterm:Holy`); To Dishonor (ID: `keyterm:Dishonor`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Exodus 31:18 (id: 11299)

* Normally, each party to a covenant received a written copy of the terms of the covenant. So it may be that the two stone tablets were identical, one being God’s copy and the other the people’s copy, to be kept together in the ark of the covenant.
* *finger of God:* See [34:28](https://ref.ly/Exod34:28).

## Exodus 32:1 (id: 11310)

The Israelites acted out of fear, disrespect for Moses, disbelief in God's leadership, and denial of responsibility. They did not want to wait for God to show his care for them. Not waiting for God often leads to sin (see [1 Samuel 13:7–13](https://ref.ly/1Sam13:7-1Sam13:13); [Isaiah 30:15–18](https://ref.ly/Isa30:15-Isa30:18)).

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 32:1

## Exodus 32:1–35 (id: 11302)

Moses had been gone for many days. While waiting at the bottom of Mount Sinai, the people wanted protection, guidance, and a way to show their worship. God understood this and wanted to help them (see chapters [25–31](https://ref.ly/Exod25:1-Exod31:18)). But the Israelites tried to meet their needs on their own. Fellowship with God requires relying on him (see [John 15:5](https://ref.ly/John15:5); [2 Corinthians 3:5](https://ref.ly/2Cor3:5)).

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 32:1-35

## Exodus 32:2–4 (id: 11311)

Aaron, who served as a priest (religious leader), asked the people to bring gold rings. After that, he did not allow them to take part in the work.

This was very different from what God commanded for building the tabernacle. In that case, the people were invited to bring many kinds of offerings as they were willing ([25:1–9](https://ref.ly/Exod25:1-Exod25:9); [35:4–36:7](https://ref.ly/Exod35:4-Exod36:7)). 

They also shared in the work under the direction of a skilled worker whom God filled with his Spirit, not under a priest acting alone ([31:1–6](https://ref.ly/Exod31:1-Exod31:6); [36:1–2](https://ref.ly/Exod36:1-Exod36:2)).

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 32:2-4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Earring (ID: `realia:Earring`); Aaron (ID: `person:Aaron`); Molten Image (ID: `realia:MoltenImage`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`)

## Exodus 32:4 (id: 11320)

* *fashioned it into a molten calf:* The idol might have been a bull, similar to the Egyptian god Amon\-Re, which the people knew from Egypt. The bull represented power, domination, and fertility. The writer might have said calf to despise the idol. Or, Aaron might have made a calf, thinking this sin was less severe than making a full\-sized bull.
* *These, O Israel, are your gods, who brought you up out:* The people credited the idol with what they had just said Moses did ([32:1](https://ref.ly/Exod32:1)). Idolatry shows the belief that the divine and visible worlds are continuous. This view suggests one can access divine power by manipulating the god through the idol.

    God insisted the opposite is true. God's creation cannot contain or limit him. No one can receive his blessing by manipulating creation, either in a ceremony or otherwise. God's blessings are for those who give up their own efforts to feel secure and come to him in the ways he has revealed.

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 32:4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Molten Image (ID: `realia:MoltenImage`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`)

## Exodus 32:5 (id: 11321)

* Aaron tried to manage the process, but he gave up leadership by simply doing what he thought people wanted.
* Although Aaron called the idol "your gods" ([32:4](https://ref.ly/Exod32:4)), he also suggested it was a physical manifestation of the Lord.

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 32:5

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Aaron (ID: `person:Aaron`); Stone Altar (ID: `realia:StoneAltar`); Tabernacle Altar (ID: `realia:TabernacleAltar`); Altar (ID: `realia:Altar`); Temple Altar (ID: `realia:TempleAltar`)

## Exodus 32:6 (id: 11322)

The Hebrew term translated as *indulge in* *revelry* is traditionally translated as *they got up to play.* In Hebrew, like in English, the word *play* can imply sexual activities (see [Genesis 26:8](https://ref.ly/Gen26:8), “caressing”). This is likely the case here. 

Worship of a fertility symbol, such as a bull, often included the worshipers engaging in sexual activities (see [1 Corinthians 10:7–8](https://ref.ly/1Cor10:7-1Cor10:8)).

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 32:6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Offering (ID: `keyterm:Offering`)

## Exodus 32:7 (id: 11323)

*your people, whom you brought up out of the land of Egypt:* HereGod attributes his own work to Moses. This suggests God was testing Moses, giving him a chance to avoid the same mistake the people made ([32:1](https://ref.ly/Exod32:1)). God was not tempting Moses to do wrong (see [James 1:13–14](https://ref.ly/Jas1:13-Jas1:14)). But he placed Moses in a situation with a clear choice that could lead in different directions (compare [1 Kings 22:19–23](https://ref.ly/1Kgs22:19-1Kgs22:23)).

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 32:7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Exodus 32:9–10 (id: 11324)

God was apparently ready to reject his people. They had broken their covenant (their binding agreement) with him.

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 32:9-10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Stubborn (ID: `keyterm:Stubborn`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Exodus 32:10 (id: 11304)

* *Now leave me alone:* This sounded like a command, but it was actually an invitation. God was giving Moses an opportunity to pray for the people. Moses was to stand before God as an intercessor (a person who speaks to God on behalf of others).

    The people deserved to be destroyed because of their sin. However, God was willing to spare them if Moses continued to pray for them.

* *I will make you into a great nation:* God told Moses that he could begin again with him alone. God could abandon the other descendants of Abraham and build a new nation from the children of Moses.

    This was likely a test. It showed whether Moses understood God’s character and his promises.

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 32:10


## Exodus 32:11–13 (id: 11305)

If there was a test, Moses passed it. He refused to take God's place ([32:11](https://ref.ly/Exod32:11)). He knew God is just and faithful and would not save people just to destroy them ([32:12](https://ref.ly/Exod32:12)). He declined the offer to become the father of a great nation because it would mean God violating promises to Abraham, Isaac, and Jacob ([32:13](https://ref.ly/Exod32:13)). Moses understood who God truly is.

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 32:11-13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Strength (ID: `keyterm:Strength`); Egyptians (ID: `group:Egypt`); Isaac (ID: `person:Isaac`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Abraham (ID: `person:Abraham`); Swear (ID: `keyterm:Swear`)

## Exodus 32:14 (id: 11306)

In response to the argument of Moses, the Lord changed his mind.

This does not show God as an angry ruler who must be forced to calm down. The Bible does not present him as a tyrant who is difficult to persuade.

Instead, the Lord is more willing to show mercy than to bring punishment. He invites those who are close to him, like Moses, to pray and speak on behalf of others.

Through faithful intercession (prayer on behalf of someone else), God gives an opportunity for mercy.

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 32:14

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Exodus 32:15–29 (id: 11307)

When Moses saw what was happening, he became much less calm than he was on the mountain. He smashed the two tablets ([32:15–19](https://ref.ly/Exod32:15-Exod32:19)). He destroyed the calf ([32:20](https://ref.ly/Exod32:20)). He confronted Aaron ([32:21–25](https://ref.ly/Exod32:21-Exod32:25)). He ordered the death of the rebellious leaders ([32:26–29](https://ref.ly/Exod32:26-Exod32:29)).

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 32:15-29

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Aaron (ID: `person:Aaron`); Tablet (ID: `realia:Tablet`); Molten Image (ID: `realia:MoltenImage`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Tribe of Levi (ID: `group:Levi`); Levi (ID: `person:Levi.3`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); Joshua (ID: `person:Joshua.2`); Testimony (ID: `keyterm:Witness.2`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); Sword (ID: `realia:Sword`)

## Exodus 32:16 (id: 11308)

*The tablets were the work of God:* The covenant was more than just a human agreement.

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 32:16

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Tablet (ID: `realia:Tablet`)

## Exodus 32:19 (id: 11309)

Moses might have shattered the tablets out of anger. It could also have been his way of saying the people shattered the covenant with God. Later, God reminded Moses that he was the one who broke the tablets (see [34:1](https://ref.ly/Exod34:1)).

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 32:19

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Molten Image (ID: `realia:MoltenImage`); Tablet (ID: `realia:Tablet`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`)

## Exodus 32:20 (id: 11312)

Passing the gold powder of the image through the bodies of the people effectively rendered it unclean.

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 32:20

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Molten Image (ID: `realia:MoltenImage`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`)

## Exodus 32:21–25 (id: 11313)

Aaron denied responsibility, even though he made the molds and poured the gold ([32:4](https://ref.ly/Exod32:4)). Aaron wanted Moses to believe it all just happened ([32:24](https://ref.ly/Exod32:24)). He implied he had no choice because the people were so evil ([32:22](https://ref.ly/Exod32:22)). 

Aaron did not fool Moses. Moses knew Aaron could have led the people, but instead, he allowed them to get completely out of control ([32:25](https://ref.ly/Exod32:25)).

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 32:21-25

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Aaron (ID: `person:Aaron`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`); Molten Image (ID: `realia:MoltenImage`)

## Exodus 32:25 (id: 11314)

*a laughingstock to their enemies:* After the Israelites’ great show of worshiping the Lord as a different kind of God, their actions proclaimed that he was merely another of the idols that their enemies had been worshiping all along (see [Ezekiel 36:19–20](https://ref.ly/Ezek36:19-Ezek36:20)).

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 32:25

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Aaron (ID: `person:Aaron`)

## Exodus 32:26–29 (id: 11315)

Moses had asked God to spare the people, but now he called on those who followed the Lord to kill those who had sinned. The Levites ([32:26](https://ref.ly/Exod32:26)) were willing to confront the sin that Aaron had let loose. Although Moses commanded them to kill everyone ([32:27](https://ref.ly/Exod32:27)), the number 3,000 ([32:28](https://ref.ly/Exod32:28)) makes it clear that the words *each of you* had a restricted meaning. 

The reference to *his son* *and his brother* suggests that as Aaron had led in the idolatry, many of the Levites had led in the worship of the idol, and they were the ones that the rest of the Levites killed. Aaron may have escaped because God had already designated him as high priest ([28:1](https://ref.ly/Exod28:1)).

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 32:26-29

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Tribe of Levi (ID: `group:Levi`); Levi (ID: `person:Levi.3`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); Sword (ID: `realia:Sword`)

## Exodus 32:30–35 (id: 11316)

This further intercession for the people may have been needed because of the new recognition Moses had of how serious the sin really was.

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 32:30-35

## Exodus 32:32 (id: 11317)

Earlier, God had offered to make Moses into a great nation instead of the people. Moses had refused that offer.

Here, Moses again showed his loyalty to the people. He asked God to destroy him also if God chose not to forgive them.

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 32:32

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); Forgive (ID: `keyterm:Forgive`); Scroll (ID: `realia:Scroll`)

## Exodus 32:33 (id: 11318)

God did not grant what Moses requested. Instead, God said he would judge only those who had sinned in that event.

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 32:33

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Scroll (ID: `realia:Scroll`)

## Exodus 32:34 (id: 11319)

* *the place I described:* This refers to Canaan, which God promised to give to Abraham’s descendants (see [3:17](https://ref.ly/Exod3:17); [32:13](https://ref.ly/Exod32:13); [33:1](https://ref.ly/Exod33:1)).
* *on the day I settle accounts:* Because of God’s mercy, judgment may not always come immediately. But in this instance, God soon sent a plague ([32:35](https://ref.ly/Exod32:35)). Judgment will come for those who continue to sin (see [Matthew 13:24–30](https://ref.ly/Matt13:24-Matt13:30)).

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 32:34

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** An Angel (ID: `deity:Angel`); To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); Appoint (ID: `keyterm:Appoint`)

## Exodus 33:1–6 (id: 11325)

God would not lead the people up to Canaan as he had previously led them (see [33:3](https://ref.ly/Exod33:3); but also [33:14–17](https://ref.ly/Exod33:14-Exod33:17)). Instead, he would send an angel ([33:2](https://ref.ly/Exod33:2)).

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 33:1-6

## Exodus 33:2–3 (id: 11335)

* *an angel … But I will not go with you:* This *angel* might not be the "angel of the LORD," since in Exodus and other parts of the Old Testament, the angel of the LORD is closely associated with God's presence (see [3:2](https://ref.ly/Exod3:2); [23:20–23](https://ref.ly/Exod23:20-Exod23:23)).

    Or, God might have meant that while he would lead them through the angel ([33:2](https://ref.ly/Exod33:2)), he would not live among them in the tabernacle as planned ([33:3](https://ref.ly/Exod33:3)).

* *Canaanites … Jebusites:* See study note on 3:8.

## Exodus 33:3 (id: 11337)

* *land flowing with milk and honey:* Canaan was not as fertile as the Nile Valley in Egypt, but it was much more productive than the Sinai desert.
* *stiff\-necked people**:* They did not keep their promises.
* *I might destroy you**:* As God of the covenant, the Lord would enforce the covenant terms that required the destruction of those who violated it (see study note on 23:20–33; also see [24:7–8](https://ref.ly/Exod24:7-Exod24:8) and study notes).

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 33:3

## Exodus 33:4–6 (id: 11338)

People might have worn jewelry and fine clothes while worshiping the gold calf. Removing them here shows mourning and repentance ([33:4](https://ref.ly/Exod33:4)).

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 33:4-6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Mount Sinai (ID: `place:SinaiMount`); Stubborn (ID: `keyterm:Stubborn`)

## Exodus 33:7 (id: 11340)

The tabernacle is often called the *Tent of Meeting* (see study note on 27:21). In many passages, these two expressions refer to the same structure.

However, in this passage, the *Tent of Meeting* refers to a different, temporary tent. Moses set up this tent outside the camp before the tabernacle was built. 

Later, when the tabernacle was completed, it was placed in the center of the camp (see [Numbers 2:1–31](https://ref.ly/Num2:1-Num2:31)).

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 33:7
* **Associated Articles:** Exodus 27:21 (ID: `11234@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Tent (ID: `realia:Tent`); Tent of Meeting (ID: `realia:TentOfMeeting`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Exodus 33:7–11 (id: 11339)

The way God communicated with his people before the completion of the tabernacle is likely described here. This helps explain the events recorded in [33:1–6](https://ref.ly/Exod33:1-Exod33:6) and [33:12–23](https://ref.ly/Exod33:12-Exod33:23).

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 33:7-11

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Tent (ID: `realia:Tent`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Joshua (ID: `person:Joshua.2`); Nun (ID: `person:Nun`); Tent of Meeting (ID: `realia:TentOfMeeting`)

## Exodus 33:8–11 (id: 11341)

*would stand … stand up and bow down:* The people considered it amazing that Moses spoke directly to God, "face to face" ([33:11](https://ref.ly/Exod33:11)).

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 33:8-11

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Tent (ID: `realia:Tent`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Joshua (ID: `person:Joshua.2`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Nun (ID: `person:Nun`)

## Exodus 33:11 (id: 11326)

Joshua had earlier gained experience as a military leader (see [17:9–13](https://ref.ly/Exod17:9-Exod17:13)). Now, he was gaining experience as a spiritual leader (see also [24:13](https://ref.ly/Exod24:13)). He may have stayed behind to guard the tent, a function the Levites would later fulfill with the tabernacle ([1 Chronicles 26](https://ref.ly/1Chr26:1-1Chr26:32)).

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 33:11

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Joshua (ID: `person:Joshua.2`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Nun (ID: `person:Nun`); Tent (ID: `realia:Tent`)

## Exodus 33:12 (id: 11328)

*I know you by name:* God chose Moses himself.

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 33:12

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Favor (ID: `keyterm:Favor`)

## Exodus 33:12–23 (id: 11327)

Moses begged God to go with him and the people to the promised land. In these verses, the word *Presence* usually translates a Hebrew word that literally means “face” ([33:14–15](https://ref.ly/Exod33:14-Exod33:15)). 

Earlier in the chapter, we learn that the Lord spoke with Moses "face to face" ([33:11](https://ref.ly/Exod33:11)). This phrase describes a close and direct relationship. It does not mean that Moses saw God’s physical face. It means that God spoke with Moses in a clear and personal way.

Moses wanted that same close relationship to continue. He did not want God to send only an angel or messenger. He wanted God’s own presence to go with them. Moses also wanted the people to share in that relationship in some way.

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 33:12-23

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Favor (ID: `keyterm:Favor`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); To Be Kindly Disposed (ID: `keyterm:KindlyDisposed`); The Way (ID: `keyterm:TheWay`)

## Exodus 33:13 (id: 11329)

* *please let me know Your ways:* Moses did not just want God's blessings. He wanted to understand God's nature and character. He also wanted to know why and how God acted. God's goal of revealing himself was starting to happen, at least with Moses.
* Moses wanted God to accompany them because they were the Lord's own people.

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 33:13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** The Way (ID: `keyterm:TheWay`); Favor (ID: `keyterm:Favor`)

## Exodus 33:14 (id: 11330)

*I will give you rest*: Literally *a place to roost,* as in a place where a bird can land and be at peace. Ever since Abraham left Ur, he and his descendants had had no such place.

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 33:14


## Exodus 33:15–16 (id: 11331)

Moses made his request more direct. He showed he understood God's actions. The exodus was not just about leaving Egypt for Canaan. It was about experiencing God personally, which would change their lives. Without this, staying at Mount Sinai would have been better. Going to Canaan without the Lord would be disastrous.

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 33:15-16

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Favor (ID: `keyterm:Favor`)

## Exodus 33:17 (id: 11332)

The Lord reaffirmed his agreement to go with them and his personal appointment of Moses.

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 33:17

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Favor (ID: `keyterm:Favor`)

## Exodus 33:18 (id: 11333)

Moses asked for confirmation of God’s promises. He asked to see God’s glorious presence (literally *glory*). He was asking for an experience of seeing the very essence of God (see study note on 16:7).

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 33:18
* **Associated Articles:** Exodus 16:7 (ID: `11091@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`)

## Exodus 33:19 (id: 11334)

* In his positive reply, God changed the terms of the request Moses made. God promised Moses a glorious experience, but he would not let Moses see his face. In the Old Testament, seeing God’s face means seeing God fully and directly.

    Instead, God said he would show Moses his goodness. He would also proclaim his name. In the Bible, a name often expresses a person’s character and identity. God’s character is shown especially in his mercy and compassion (see [Romans 9:14–16](https://ref.ly/Rom9:14-Rom9:16)).

* *the LORD*: In many English Bibles, the word LORD (written in capital letters) stands for the personal name of God, *Yahweh*.

    In this verse, God emphasizes his personal name. This name shows that he is not distant. He is the covenant God (the God who makes promises) of his people.

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 33:19

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** To Be Kindly Disposed (ID: `keyterm:KindlyDisposed`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Exodus 33:20–23 (id: 11336)

God desires to reveal his character to his people. However, to see his face means to see his full being (his true and complete nature). No human can see God in this way and live.

God’s glorious presence is described in [33:22](https://ref.ly/Exod33:22). That kind of direct encounter would bring death to a human person. God allowed Moses to see only a small part of his glory. Moses was given a brief glimpse, but nothing more.

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 33:20-23

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`)

## Exodus 34:1–3 (id: 11343)

God called Moses to come back up Mount Sinai with two new stone tablets. The restrictions for the rest of the people are like those made at first (see [19:12–13](https://ref.ly/Exod19:12-Exod19:13), [21–25](https://ref.ly/Exod19:21-Exod19:25)).

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 34:1-3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Mount Sinai (ID: `place:SinaiMount`); Cattle (ID: `fauna:Bull`); Goat (ID: `fauna:Goat`); Tablet (ID: `realia:Tablet`); Flock (ID: `fauna:Flock`)

## Exodus 34:1–35 (id: 11342)

God granted the request of Moses ([33:18](https://ref.ly/Exod33:18)). He showed Moses his goodness ([34:1–9](https://ref.ly/Exod34:1-Exod34:9)). He renewed the covenant ([34:10–35](https://ref.ly/Exod34:10-Exod34:35)). 

This renewal was a statement made by God alone. God promised to stay with his people, keeping his covenant promises even though they had broken the covenant and deserved death and abandonment ([32:1–6](https://ref.ly/Exod32:1-Exod32:6)).

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 34:1-35

## Exodus 34:5–6 (id: 11355)

*the LORD*: This represents the personal name of God, *Yahweh*. This name appears five times in this passage. By repeating this name, God emphasizes his personal name. This shows that he is speaking and acting as the covenant God (the God who makes promises) of his people.

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 34:5-6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Kindness (ID: `keyterm:Kindness`); Truth (ID: `keyterm:Truth`); Generous (ID: `keyterm:Generous`); Compassionate (ID: `keyterm:Compassionate`)

## Exodus 34:5–9 (id: 11356)

Moses experienced God’s presence in a revelation of the name*,* or character, of God. As God had promised ([33:19](https://ref.ly/Exod33:19)), he showed Moses the glory of his goodness.

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 34:5-9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Kindness (ID: `keyterm:Kindness`); To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); Passion (ID: `keyterm:Ardour`); Forgive (ID: `keyterm:Forgive`); Truth (ID: `keyterm:Truth`); Generous (ID: `keyterm:Generous`); Pronounce Innocent (ID: `keyterm:PronounceInnocent`); Compassionate (ID: `keyterm:Compassionate`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Rebel (ID: `keyterm:Rebel`); Favor (ID: `keyterm:Favor`); Stubborn (ID: `keyterm:Stubborn`); Appoint (ID: `keyterm:Appoint`)

## Exodus 34:6 (id: 11357)

*compassionate and gracious* (see [20:5–6](https://ref.ly/Exod20:5-Exod20:6); [33:19](https://ref.ly/Exod33:19) and study notes): The Creator who was revealing himself to the Israelites, and through them to the world, is a God of grace. Neither his justice nor his sovereignty are underlined here, as true as those attributes are. If God were merely just, the Old Testament would have ended at this point, or it would have picked up and started again with an entirely new family. 

This did not happen because God, in his unique character, is slow to anger and full of unfailing love and faithfulness. These qualities were the basis for his renewal of the covenant.

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 34:6
* **Associated Articles:** Exodus 20:5-6 (ID: `11155@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Kindness (ID: `keyterm:Kindness`); Truth (ID: `keyterm:Truth`); Generous (ID: `keyterm:Generous`); Compassionate (ID: `keyterm:Compassionate`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Exodus 34:7 (id: 11358)

* God’s unfailing love and his generous desire to forgive are not weakness or indecisiveness, nor are they reason to sin. Sin will have its effects, because God created a world of cause and effect. The murderer may repent, be forgiven, and lead a new life, but the effects of previous choices will continue to play out. We should not sin just because we know that God will forgive (see study note on 20:5–6).
* *a thousand generations:* See [Deuteronomy 7:9–11](https://ref.ly/Deut7:9-Deut7:11).
* *He will visit the iniquity of the fathers:* Our sins affect future generations, but God limits the effects to three or four generations. See also [Exodus 20:5–6](https://ref.ly/Exod20:5-Exod20:6).

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 34:7
* **Associated Articles:** Exodus 20:5-6 (ID: `11155@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Pronounce Innocent (ID: `keyterm:PronounceInnocent`); To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); Kindness (ID: `keyterm:Kindness`); Passion (ID: `keyterm:Ardour`); Rebel (ID: `keyterm:Rebel`); Forgive (ID: `keyterm:Forgive`); Appoint (ID: `keyterm:Appoint`)

## Exodus 34:8–9 (id: 11359)

Since the Lord had already twice promised to go with them ([33:14](https://ref.ly/Exod33:14), [17](https://ref.ly/Exod33:17))this renewed request seems to show a lack of faith. It may also be that God’s presence had driven home to Moses how absolutely holy God is and how very different he is from this stubborn and rebellious people. 

Here, Moses took his request a step further and asked God to travel with the people and make them his own treasured possession (see [19:5–6](https://ref.ly/Exod19:5-Exod19:6)). Moses asked for the complete restoration of the relationship.

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 34:8-9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Passion (ID: `keyterm:Ardour`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Favor (ID: `keyterm:Favor`); Forgive (ID: `keyterm:Forgive`); Stubborn (ID: `keyterm:Stubborn`); To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`)

## Exodus 34:10–11 (id: 11344)

God promised to miraculously care for his people as he brought them into Canaan. This care depended on their obedience. In Hebrew, the word translated as *observe* also means "obey"([34:11](https://ref.ly/Exod34:11))*.* Unlike English, Hebrew does not separate hearing a command from obeying it.

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 34:10-11

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Perizzites (ID: `group:Perizzites`); Hivites (ID: `group:Hivites`); Jebusites (ID: `group:Jebusites`); Heth (ID: `person:Heth`); Canaanites (ID: `group:Canaan`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Hittites (ID: `group:Hittite`); Canaan (ID: `place:Canaan`); Amorites (ID: `group:Amorites`); Covenant (ID: `keyterm:Covenant`)

## Exodus 34:12–17 (id: 11346)

Worship of idols was prohibited, particularly the idols of the peoples into whose lands the Israelites were going. A key part of this restriction was the ban on treaties, or covenants, with these idol\-worshiping peoples. Making a treaty would mean acknowledging their gods, as ancient treaties often called upon gods as witnesses (see study note on 20:1–23:33). 

The treaties would also tempt the Israelites to adopt the customs of their treaty partners ([34:12](https://ref.ly/Exod34:12), [15–16](https://ref.ly/Exod34:15-Exod34:16)).

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 34:12-17
* **Associated Articles:** Exodus 20:1-23.33 (ID: `11137@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Covenant (ID: `keyterm:Covenant`); Play the Prostitute (ID: `keyterm:PlayTheProstitute`); Jealous (ID: `keyterm:Zealous.2`); Slaughtering (ID: `keyterm:Slaughtering`); Asherah (ID: `deity:Asherah`); Sacred Pole (ID: `realia:SacredPole`); Trap (ID: `realia:Trap`); Stone Altar (ID: `realia:StoneAltar`); Sacred Pillar (ID: `realia:SacredPillar`); Tabernacle Altar (ID: `realia:TabernacleAltar`); Altar (ID: `realia:Altar`); Temple Altar (ID: `realia:TempleAltar`); Molten Image (ID: `realia:MoltenImage`)

## Exodus 34:13 (id: 11347)

* Asherah was a Canaanite fertility goddess who would continue to be a temptation throughout Israel’s history (see, for example, [Judges 6:25](https://ref.ly/Judg6:25); [1 Kings 14:15](https://ref.ly/1Kgs14:15); [2 Kings 23:15](https://ref.ly/2Kgs23:15)).
* The Aherah poles seem to have been fertility symbols.

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 34:13

## Exodus 35:1–40.38 (id: 11360)

Much of the report about building the Tent of Meeting repeats what [25:1–31:18](https://ref.ly/Exod25:1-Exod31:18) said. There is a change from the imperative mood to the indicative, and there are certain changes in the order. 

This repetition emphasizes that the work was done exactly as God commanded (see [40:16](https://ref.ly/Exod40:16)). The people had tried to secure God's presence their own way with the gold calf, which led to disaster. Now, by following God's way, they received blessings ([40:34–38](https://ref.ly/Exod40:34-Exod40:38)).

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 35:4-36:38

## Exodus 35:4–36.7 (id: 11361)

At the creation of the gold calf, Aaron asked only for earrings (see study note on 32:2–4). Here, Moses invites the people to bring various gifts ([35:4–9](https://ref.ly/Exod35:4-Exod35:9)). They gave as their hearts and spirits inspired them ([35:21](https://ref.ly/Exod35:21)). Everyone could contribute something and the giving was voluntary. So, the people ended up giving too much and Moses had to stop them ([36:4–7](https://ref.ly/Exod36:4-Exod36:7)).

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 35:4-36:7

It says that Bezalel made the ark of the covenant and all the furnishings, as well as the courtyard and its furnishings ([37:1](https://ref.ly/Exod37:1), [10](https://ref.ly/Exod37:10), [17](https://ref.ly/Exod37:17), [25](https://ref.ly/Exod37:25); [38:1](https://ref.ly/Exod38:1), [8](https://ref.ly/Exod38:8), [9](https://ref.ly/Exod38:9), [18](https://ref.ly/Exod38:18)). But this probably means he was in charge and guided the other craftsmen and seamstresses.

## Exodus 38:8 (id: 11367)

* The plans for the bronze basin were part of the instructions to the priests ([30:17–21](https://ref.ly/Exod30:17-Exod30:21); [29:38–30:38](https://ref.ly/Exod29:38-Exod30:38)). Here, however, the text reports how the basin was built, following a logical order.
* This bronze, which they could polish to look like mirrors, was probably of very high quality. It was suitable for use in God's Tent of Meeting.

## Exodus 38:21 (id: 11365)

Since the tabernacle contained the ark of the covenant (also called the ark of the Testimony), it is called the *tabernacle of the Testimony*.

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 38:21

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Testimony (ID: `keyterm:Witness.2`); Tribe of Levi (ID: `group:Levi`); Ithamar (ID: `person:Ithamar`); Aaron (ID: `person:Aaron`); Levi (ID: `person:Levi.3`)

## Exodus 38:21–29 (id: 11364)

The very large amount of metal listed here includes more than one metric ton (about 1,000 kilograms) of gold, almost four metric tons (about 4,000 kilograms) of silver, and two and a half metric tons (about 2,500 kilograms) of bronze.

This large supply shows how eager the Egyptians were to send the Israelites away. The Egyptians gave the Israelites whatever they asked for so that they would leave quickly (see [12:35–36](https://ref.ly/Exod12:35-Exod12:36)).

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 38:21-29

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Holy (ID: `keyterm:Holy`); Talent (ID: `realia:Talent`); Shekel (ID: `realia:Shekel`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Offering (ID: `keyterm:Offering`); Money (ID: `realia:Money`); Oholiab (ID: `person:Oholiab`); Ahisamach (ID: `person:Ahisamach`); Uri (ID: `person:Uri`); Hur (ID: `person:Hur.2`); Tribe of Dan (ID: `group:Dan`); Aaron (ID: `person:Aaron`); Dan (ID: `place:Dan`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Bezalel (ID: `person:Bezalel`); Levi (ID: `person:Levi.3`); Judah (ID: `person:Judah.4`); Testimony (ID: `keyterm:Witness.2`); Tribe of Levi (ID: `group:Levi`); Ithamar (ID: `person:Ithamar`); Tribe of Judah (ID: `group:Judah`); A Half Shekel (ID: `realia:AHalfShekel`); Craftsman (ID: `realia:Craftsman`); Embroidered Cloth (ID: `realia:EmbroideredCloth`); Purple Cloth (ID: `realia:PurpleCloth`); Curtain (ID: `realia:Curtain`); Linen (ID: `realia:Linen`)

## Exodus 39:32–43 (id: 11370)

Moses inspected the completed work and confirmed that everything was as the Lord had commanded ([39:43](https://ref.ly/Exod39:43)). As a result, Moses blessed them instead of judging them. This was unlike when they tried to enter God's presence in their own ways ([32:27](https://ref.ly/Exod32:27)).

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 39:32-43

## Exodus 40:1–33 (id: 11387)

* The report on setting up the Tent of Meeting starts with the Lord's commands ([40:1–15](https://ref.ly/Exod40:1-Exod40:15)). It continues with how Moses obeyed those commands ([40:16–33](https://ref.ly/Exod40:16-Exod40:33)).
* The repeated statement that "Moses did everything just as the LORD had commanded" ([40:16](https://ref.ly/Exod40:16), [19](https://ref.ly/Exod40:19), [21](https://ref.ly/Exod40:21), [23](https://ref.ly/Exod40:23), [25](https://ref.ly/Exod40:25), [27](https://ref.ly/Exod40:27), [29](https://ref.ly/Exod40:29), [32](https://ref.ly/Exod40:32)) strongly contrasts with Aaron’s self\-serving statement in [32:22–24](https://ref.ly/Exod32:22-Exod32:24). God wants to share his presence with us, but his holiness requires us to give up our own efforts to secure his presence and gifts.

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 40:1-33

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Tent of Meeting (ID: `realia:TentOfMeeting`); Smear (ID: `keyterm:Smear`); Altar (ID: `realia:Altar`); Temple Altar (ID: `realia:TempleAltar`); Stone Altar (ID: `realia:StoneAltar`); Tabernacle Altar (ID: `realia:TabernacleAltar`); Screen (ID: `realia:Screen`); Holy (ID: `keyterm:Holy`); Testimony (ID: `keyterm:Witness.2`); Covenant Box (ID: `keyterm:CovenantBox`); Ark of the Covenant (ID: `realia:ArkOfTheCovenant`); Curtain (ID: `realia:Curtain`); Aaron (ID: `person:Aaron`); Offering (ID: `keyterm:Offering`); Priesthood (ID: `keyterm:Priesthood`); Lamp (ID: `realia:Lamp`); Lampstand (ID: `realia:Lampstand.2`); Frame (ID: `realia:Frame`); Tent (ID: `realia:Tent`); Olive Oil (ID: `realia:OliveOil`); Cover Lid (ID: `realia:CoverLid`); Covering (ID: `realia:Covering`); Anointing Oil (ID: `realia:AnointingOil`); Tunic (ID: `realia:Tunic`)

## Exodus 40:2 (id: 11388)

* In the Tent of Meeting, God met with Israel to communicate his will to them.
* On the first day of the new year, they set up the Tent of Meeting. This was one year after God announced the Passover and the beginning of Israel's calendar.

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 40:2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Tent of Meeting (ID: `realia:TentOfMeeting`)

## Exodus 40:3 (id: 11392)

*Testimony* (Hebrew *‘eduth*):This word refers especially to the covenant laws (stipulations, meaning the required commands).

A written copy of these laws was placed inside the ark. This copy served as a witness to the covenant that Israel had made with God.

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 40:3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Covenant Box (ID: `keyterm:CovenantBox`); Testimony (ID: `keyterm:Witness.2`); Curtain (ID: `realia:Curtain`); Ark of the Covenant (ID: `realia:ArkOfTheCovenant`)

## Exodus 40:33 (id: 11389)

*So Moses finished the work*: This statement marks the completion of the work on the tabernacle. The long process of building and preparing all its parts had now come to an end. See study note on 37:1–29.

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 40:33
* **Associated Articles:** Exodus 37:1-29 (ID: `11362@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Stone Altar (ID: `realia:StoneAltar`); Tabernacle Altar (ID: `realia:TabernacleAltar`); Screen (ID: `realia:Screen`); Altar (ID: `realia:Altar`); Temple Altar (ID: `realia:TempleAltar`)

## Exodus 40:34–38 (id: 11390)

The most important part of the exodus happened when the glory of the Lord filled the Tent of Meeting.

From the beginning, God’s purpose was not only to rescue Israel from slavery. He wanted to bring them into a close relationship with himself. Their greatest need was to know God personally.

Without God’s presence, the promised land would not truly be a place of blessing. But now it would be different. The same glory that appeared on Mount Sinai was now with Israel. It would not stay behind. It would travel with them wherever they went.

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 40:34-38

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Tent of Meeting (ID: `realia:TentOfMeeting`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`)

## Exodus 40:38 (id: 11391)

*all the house of Israel:* This description reminds us of how the book of Exodus began (see [1:1–7](https://ref.ly/Exod1:1-Exod1:7)). At that time, Jacob’s family had grown into a large group in Egypt. Later, they became slaves there. This was a dramatic change. They were no longer slaves. Now they were free.

God had promised Abraham that his descendants would bring blessing to all the nations ([Genesis 12:3](https://ref.ly/Gen12:3)).

* **Associated Passages:** Exodus 40:38

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`)
